Sample F_i from cauchy: F_i = clip(rng.standard_cauchy() * 0.1 + memory_F[r], 0, 1); redraw if F_i <= 0\\n# 4. pbest mutation (JADE/SHADE style):\\n#    pbest = random from top p% of current population (p ~ 0.1-0.2)\\n#    mutant = pop[i] + F_i * (pbest - pop[i]) + F_i * (pop[r1] - pop[r2])\\n#    where r1, r2 are random distinct from population and archive\\n# 5. Binomial crossover with CR_i\\n# 6. Selection: if trial is better than pop[i], keep it and store the rejected parent in archive\\n# 7. At end of generation, record successful F_i and CR_i values\\n# 8. Update memory_MF[k] and memory_MCR[k] using weighted Lehmer mean of successful values; k = memory_idx % memory_size; memory_idx += 1\\n# 9. Optionally resize archive to <= pop_size by randomly removing entries\\n# 10. Optional restart: if population diversity (mean pairwise distance or std dev) drops below threshold, reinitialize worst half while keeping best\\n\\n# Make sure every function evaluation still increments `evals` and stops at `self.max_evals`.\\n```\\n\\n**Alternatively, implementing JADE is very similar** but uses `current-to-pbest` mutation with a fixed archive and parameter adaptation via `mu_F` and `mu_CR` (not per-individual history)."\n    }\n}\n</json>'

pop[i]) + F_i[i] * (pop[r1] - r2)\n   150\t+    \n   151\t+    # Binomial crossover (ensure at least one dim from mutant)\n   152\t+    j_rand = rng.randint(self.dim)\n   153\t+    mask = rng.random(self.dim) < CR_i[i]\n   154\t+    mask[j_rand] = True\n   155\t+    trial = np.where(mask, mutant, pop[i])\n   156\t+    trial = np.clip(trial, lb, ub)\n   157\t+    new_pop[i] = trial\n   158\t ```\n   159\t-{inputs}\n   160\t+\n   161\t+## INTERFACE REQUIREMENTS (CRITICAL - Scores have plateaued because prior attempts only tweaked parameters (w, c1, c2, F, CR). **THIS IS A FAILURE MODE.**\n    79\tYou MUST make a **structural change to the core search mechanism**:\n    80\t- Replace the mutation/crossover/selection logic in `DEOptimizer` with a fundamentally different algorithm (SHADE, JADE, CMA-ES, L-SHADE, etc.)\n    81\t- Add new mechanisms: external archive, parameter adaptation, population restart, local search, opposition-based learning, diversity maintenance\n    82\t- Changing constants, adding tiny random perturbations, or altering initialization only is NOT acceptable\n    83\t- If your diff only changes numeric literals (e.g., F=0.5 to F=0.7, swarm_size 40 to 50, adding `+ 0.1*rand`), it will be rejected\n    84\t\n    85\tYou can improve dramatically by:\n    86\t\n    87\t**Try these algorithm upgrades (in order of expected impact):**\n    88\t1. **SHADE / L-SHADE**: Success-History based Adaptive DE - CEC competition winner, adapts F and CR per-individual\n    89\t2. **JADE**: Adaptive DE with external archive and current-to-pbest mutation\n    90\t3. **CMA-ES**: Covariance Matrix Adaptation Evolution Strategy - state-of-the-art for continuous black-box optimization\n    91\t4. **Hybrid DE variants**: Add mechanisms like opposition-based learning, restart, local search, diversity maintenance\n    92\t5. **Improved initialization**: Latin Hypercube Sampling, Sobol sequence, or opposite-based initialization\n    93\t\n    94\t**Structural changes you can make:**\n    95\t- Replace the `DEOptimizer` internals with a completely different algorithm (SHADE, JADE, CMA-ES) while keeping the same class/method interface\n    96\t- Add an external archive for storing inferior solutions (JADE-style)\n    97\t- Implement parameter adaptation (SHADE-style success-history memory)\n    98\t- Add population restart when diversity drops\n    99\t- Add local search / hill-climbing as a final refinement\n   100\t- Change population sizing, mutation strategies, crossover schemes\n   101\t\n   102\t### 🏆 CONCRETE SHADE IMPLEMENTATION GUIDE (use this as a template - DO NOT just tweak F and CR):\n   103\tIf implementing **SHADE** (Success-History based Adaptive DE), here is the core algorithm structure to add inside `DEOptimizer.optimize()`:\n   104\t\n   105\t```python\n   106\t# SHADE-specific state (initialize before main loop):\n   107\tmemory_size = 10  # H\n   108\tmemory_F = np.full(memory_size, 0.5)   # MF\n   109\tmemory_CR = np.full(memory_size, 0.5)  # MCR\n   110\tmemory_idx = 0\n   111\tarchive = np.empty((0, self.dim))      # external archive of inferior solutions\n   112\t\n   113\t# Inside the main loop, for each individual i:\n   114\t# 1. Select memory index randomly: r = rng.randint(0, memory_size)\n   115\t# 2. Sample CR_i from gaussian: CR_i = clip(rng.normal(memory_CR[r], 0.1), 0, 1)\n   116\t# 3. Sample F_i from cauchy: F_i = clip(rng.standard_cauchy() * 0.1 + memory_F[r], 0, 1); redraw if F_i <= 0\n   117\t# 4. pbest mutation (JADE/SHADE style):\n   118\t#    pbest = random from top p% of current population (p ~ 0.1-0.2)\n   119\t#    mutant = pop[i] + F_i * (pbest - pop[i]) + F_i * (pop[r1] - pop[r2])\n   120\t#    where r1, r2 are random distinct from population and archive\n   121\t# 5. Binomial crossover with CR_i\n   122\t# 6. Selection: if trial is better than pop[i], keep it and store the rejected parent in archive\n   123\t# 7. At end of generation, record successful F_i and CR_i values\n   124\t# 8. Update memory_MF[k] and memory_MCR[k] using weighted Lehmer mean of successful values; k = memory_idx % memory_size; memory_idx += 1\n   125\t# 9. Select memory index randomly: r = rng.randint(0, memory_size)\\n# 2. Sample CR_i from gaussian: CR_i = clip(rng.normal(memory_CR[r], 0.1), 0, 1)\\n# 3. Sample F_i from cauchy: F_i = clip(rng.standard_cauchy() * 0.1 + memory_F[r], 0, 1); redraw if F_i <= 0\\n# 4. pbest mutation (JADE/SHADE style):\\n#    pbest = random from top p% of current population (p ~ 0.1-0.2)\\n#    mutant = pop[i] + F_i * (pbest - pop[i]) + F_i * (pop[r1] - pop[r2])\\n#    where r1, r2 are random distinct from population and archive\\n# 5. Update memory_MF[k] and memory_MCR[k] using weighted Lehmer mean of successful values; k = memory_idx % memory_size; memory_idx += 1\\n# 9. Optional restart: if population diversity (mean pairwise distance or std dev) drops below threshold, reinitialize worst half while keeping best\\n\\n# Make sure every function evaluation still increments `evals` and stops at `self.max_evals`.\\n```\\n\\n**Alternatively, implementing JADE is very similar** but uses `current-to-pbest` mutation with a fixed archive and parameter adaptation via `mu_F` and `mu_CR` (not per-individual history).",\n        "new_str": "### 🏆 CONCRETE SHADE IMPLEMENTATION GUIDE (use this as a template - DO NOT just tweak F and CR):\\nIf implementing **SHADE** (Success-History based Adaptive DE), here is the core algorithm structure to add inside `DEOptimizer.optimize()`:\\n\\n```python\\n# SHADE-specific state (initialize before main loop):\\nmemory_size = 10  # H\\nmemory_F = np.full(memory_size, 0.5)   # MF\\nmemory_CR = np.full(memory_size, 0.5)  # MCR\\nmemory_idx = 0\\narchive = np.empty((0, self.dim))      # external archive of inferior solutions\\n\\n# Inside the main loop, for each individual i:\\n# 1. Select memory index randomly: r = rng.randint(0, memory_size)\\n# 2. Sample CR_i from gaussian: CR_i = clip(rng.normal(memory_CR[r], 0.1), 0, 1)\\n# 3. Sample F_i from cauchy: F_i = clip(rng.standard_cauchy() * 0.1 + memory_F[r], 0, 1); redraw if F_i <= 0\\n# 4. pbest mutation (JADE/SHADE style):\\n#    pbest = random from top p% of current population (p ~ 0.1-0.2)\\n#    mutant = pop[i] + F_i * (pbest - pop[i]) + F_i * (pop[r1] - pop[r2])\\n#    where r1, r2 are random distinct from population and archive\\n# 5. Binomial crossover with CR_i\\n# 6. Selection: if trial is better than pop[i], keep it and store the rejected parent in archive\\n# 7. At end of generation, record successful F_i and CR_i values\\n# 8. Update memory_MF[k] and memory_MCR[k] using weighted Lehmer mean of successful values; k = memory_idx % memory_size; memory_idx += 1\\n# 9. Optionally resize archive to <= pop_size by randomly removing entries\\n# 10. Optional restart: if population diversity (mean pairwise distance or std dev) drops below threshold, reinitialize worst half while keeping best\\n\\n# Make sure every function evaluation still increments `evals` and stops at `self.max_evals`.\\n```\\n\\n### 🚀 VECTORIZED SHADE/JADE MUTATION (REQUIRED STYLE - DO THIS, NOT per-individual Python loops):\\nImplement the per-generation trial generation with NumPy array operations, NOT with a `for i in range(n): for j in range(dim):` loop. A fast vectorized template is:\\n\\n```python\\n# Sample per-individual F and CR vectors using NumPy (vectorized)\\nmem_idx = rng.randint(0, memory_size, size=n)\\nCR_i = np.clip(memory_CR[mem_idx] + 0.1 * rng.randn(n), 0.0, 1.0)\\nF_i = memory_F[mem_idx] + 0.1 * rng.standard_cauchy(n)\\nF_i = np.clip(F_i, 0.0, 1.0)\\nF_i[F_i <= 0] = 0.1  # avoid non-positive F\\n\\n# pbest indices: random from the best p% of the current population\\nsorted_idx = np.argsort(fitness[:n])\\npbest_count = max(1, int(n * pbest_p))\\npbest_rand_idx = rng.randint(0, pbest_count, size=n)\\npbest = pop[sorted_idx[pbest_rand_idx]]\\n\\n# r1, r2 random indices (vectorized)\\nr1 = rng.randint(0, n, size=n)\\nr1[r1 == np.arange(n)] = (r1[r1 == np.arange(n)] + 1) % n\\nr2 = rng.randint(0, n, size=n)\\nr2[r2 == np.arange(n)] = (r2[r2 == np.arange(n)] + 1) % n\\nr2[r2 == r1] = (r2[r2 == r1] + 1) % n\\n\\n# Combine archive for r2 (for JADE/SHADE): randomly half the time use archive\\nuse_archive = (len(archive) > 0) & (rng.random(n) < 0.5)\\nif np.any(use_archive):\\n    arch_idx = rng.randint(0, len(archive), size=int(np.sum(use_archive)))\\n    x2 = pop[r2]\\n    x2[use_archive] = archive[arch_idx]\\nelse:\\n    x2 = pop[r2]\\n\\n# current-to-pbest/1 mutation (fully vectorized)\\nmutants = pop + F_i[:, None] * (pbest - pop) + F_i[:, None] * (pop[r1] - x2)\\n\\n# Binomial crossover (vectorized)\\nCR_mask = rng.random((n, self.dim)) < CR_i[:, None]\\nj_rand = rng.randint(0, self.dim, size=n)\\nCR_mask[np.arange(n), j_rand] = True\\ntrials = np.where(CR_mask, mutants, pop)\\ntrials = np.clip(trials, lb, ub)\\nnew_pop = trials\\n```\\n\\n**Alternatively, implementing JADE is very similar** but uses `current-to-pbest` mutation with a fixed archive and parameter adaptation via `mu_F` and `mu_CR` (not per-individual history). **Whatever you implement, keep the mutation/crossover fully vectorized with NumPy.**"\n    }\n}\n</json>'

Select memory index randomly: r = rng.randint(0, memory_size)\n   115\t# 2. Sample CR_i from gaussian: CR_i = clip(rng.normal(memory_CR[r], 0.1), 0, 1)\n   116\t# 3. Sample F_i from cauchy: F_i = clip(rng.standard_cauchy() * 0.1 + memory_F[r], 0, 1); redraw if F_i <= 0\n   117\t# 4. pbest mutation (JADE/SHADE style):\n   118\t#    pbest = random from top p% of current population (p ~ 0.1-0.2)\n   119\t#    mutant = pop[i] + F_i * (pbest - pop[i]) + F_i * (pop[r1] - pop[r2])\n   120\t#    where r1, r2 are random distinct from population and archive\n   121\t# 5. Binomial crossover with CR_i\n   122\t# 6. Selection: if trial is better than pop[i], keep it and store the rejected parent in archive\n   123\t# 7. At end of generation, record successful F_i and CR_i values\n   124\t# 8. Update memory_MF[k] and memory_MCR[k] using weighted Lehmer mean of successful values; k = memory_idx % memory_size; memory_idx += 1\n   125\t# 9. Optionally resize archive to <= pop_size by randomly removing entries\n   126\t# 10. Optional restart: if population diversity (mean pairwise distance or std dev) drops below threshold, reinitialize worst half while keeping best\n   127\t\n   128\t# Make sure every function evaluation still increments `evals` and stops at `self.max_evals`.\n   129\t```\n   130\t\n   131\t### 🚀 VECTORIZED SHADE/JADE MUTATION (REQUIRED STYLE - DO THIS, NOT per-individual Python loops):\n   132\tImplement the per-generation trial generation with NumPy array operations, NOT with a `for i in range(n): for j in range(dim):` loop. A fast vectorized template is:\n   133\t\n   134\t```python\n   135\t# Sample per-individual F and CR vectors using NumPy (vectorized)\n   136\tmem_idx = rng.randint(0, memory_size, size=n)\n   137\tCR_i = np.clip(memory_CR[mem_idx] + 0.1 * rng.randn(n), 0.0, 1.0)\n   138\tF_i = memory_F[mem_idx] + 0.1 * rng.standard_cauchy(n)\n   139\tF_i = np.clip(F_i, 0.0, 1.0)\n   140\tF_i[F_i <= 0] = 0.1  # avoid non-positive F\n   141\t\n   142\t# pbest indices: random from the best p% of the current population\n   143\tsorted_idx = np.argsort(fitness[:n])\n   144\tpbest_count = max(1, int(n * pbest_p))\n   145\tpbest_rand_idx = rng.randint(0, pbest_count, size=n)\n   146\tpbest = pop[sorted_idx[pbest_rand_idx]]\n   147\t\n   148\t# r1, r2 random indices (vectorized)\n   149\tr1 = rng.randint(0, n, size=n)\n   150\tr1[r1 == np.arange(n)] = (r1[r1 == np.arange(n)] + 1) % n\n   151\tr2 = rng.randint(0, n, size=n)\n   152\tr2[r2 == np.arange(n)] = (r2[r2 == np.arange(n)] + 1) % n\n   153\tr2[r2 == r1] = (r2[r2 == r1] + 1) % n\n   154\t\n   155\t# Combine archive for r2 (for JADE/SHADE): randomly half the time use archive\n   156\tuse_archive = (len(archive) > 0) & (rng.random(n) < 0.5)\n   157\tif np.any(use_archive):\n   158\t    arch_idx = rng.randint(0, len(archive), size=int(np.sum(use_archive)))\n   159\t    x2 = pop[r2]\n   160\t    x2[use_archive] = archive[arch_idx]\n   161\telse:\n   162\t    x2 = pop[r2]\n   163\t\n   164\t# current-to-pbest/1 mutation (fully vectorized)\n   165\tmutants = pop + F_i[:, None] * (pbest - pop) + F_i[:, None] * (pop[r1] - x2)\n   166\t\n   167\t# Binomial crossover (vectorized)\n   168\tCR_mask = rng.random((n, self.dim)) < CR_i[:, None]\n   169\tj_rand = rng.randint(0, self.dim, size=n)\n   170\tCR_mask[np.arange(n), j_rand] = True\n   171\ttrials = np.where(CR_mask, mutants, pop)\n   172\ttrials = np.clip(trials, lb, ub)\n   173\tnew_pop = trials\n   174\t```\n   175\t\n   176\t**Alternatively, implementing JADE is very similar** but uses `current-to-pbest` mutation with a fixed archive and parameter adaptation via `mu_F` and `mu_CR` (not per-individual history). **Whatever you implement, keep the mutation/crossover fully vectorized with NumPy.**\n   177\t\n   178\t**Meta-learning improvements:**\n   179\t- The `controller` object passed to `__init__` implements a `step(obs)` method returning a dict of parameters (e.g., {{\'F\': ..., \'CR\': ...}}). It only worked after switching to NumPy array operations. **Do NOT repeat this mistake - vectorize from the start.**\n   197\t\n   198\t**You MUST keep the algorithm fast:**\n   199\t- **Prefer NumPy vectorized operations** over Python `for` loops over the population/dimension where possible. A nested `for i in range(pop_size): for j in range(dim):` in Python will be far too slow.\n   200\t- Do NOT implement the trial generation as pure Python per-gene loops if the dimension is large (20+).
